progress with a safe commit checkpoint\n- lean4:disprove: Guided counterexample search with certified refutation\n- lean4:doctor: Diagnostics, cleanup, and migration help\n- lean4:draft: Draft Lean declaration skeletons from informal claims\n- lean4:formalize: Interactive formalization — drafting plus guided proving\n- lean4:golf: Improve Lean proofs for directness, clarity, performance, and brevity\n- lean4:learn: Interactive teaching and mathlib exploration\n- lean4:prove: Guided cycle-by-cycle theorem proving with explicit checkpoints\n- lean4:refactor: Leverage mathlib, extract helpers, simplify proof strategies\n- lean4:review: Read-only code review of Lean proofs\n- skeft-qa:frontier: Surface the derived atlas frontier — the project's OPEN assumptions ranked by how much each gates, AND the NEGATIVE frontier (settled-dead forks to avoid) — so fan-out targets high-leverage provable work and steers clear of kernel-checked dead paths. Read-only.\n- skeft-qa:notebook: Lab-notebook lifecycle ops for a /goal loop — new (bootstrap a correct active notebook + INDEX), sync (refresh the mechanical INDEX scaffold + flag staleness), shard (self-level the active shard under the ~25k-token budget), roll (freeze the whole active shard at a wave boundary / blob escape), check (read-only sizes + warnings). Auto-resolves the loop's notebook home from the marker if you omit it.\n- skeft-qa:orient: Re-orient on the current managed dev loop — a ≤200-word compass from the goal marker + source-of-truth docs (where am I, what's the next brick).\n- skeft-qa:reset-slot: Reset an UNLEASED parallel Lean worktree slot (wt1/wt2/wt3) to current main — the guardrail-safe way (checkout -B, refuses if the slot has commits not yet on main) and auto-re-clones the slot's .lake build when main advanced. For a leased slot use `slotctl prepare --slot N`, which also verifies the build epoch.\n- learn-anything:learn: Start or continue learning any skill — the primary interface to the meta-learning system.\n- learn-anything:materials: Generate or regenerate learning materials for the active skill\n- learn-anything:train: Jump straight into a training session for your active skill.\n- learn-anything:update: Update an existing curriculum to reflect new developments in the field\n- architecture-patterns:architecture-patterns: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"design system architecture\", \"split monolith\", \"choose database\", \"design API\", \"service boundaries\", \"microservices vs monolith\", \"data flow design\", \"REST API design\", \"scale application\", \"define service contracts\", or needs guidance on software architecture patterns, service design, data architecture, API contracts, and scaling decisions.\n- bhappy-repo-expert:bhappy-repo-sme: This skill should be used when an agent asks \"what module handles X?\", \"how does Y work in this codebase?\", \"where is Z implemented?\", \"why did we choose X?\", \"have we tried Y before?\", \"would X be compatible?\", \"what's the current state of feature Y?\", \"when was X added?\", or needs codebase context for implementing features. Provides Subject Matter Expert knowledge for the BHappy repository including architecture, decision history, compatibility analysis, and progressive disclosure for efficient context bootstrapping. Part of the bhappy-repo-expert plugin for BHappy self-development.\n- brainstorming:brainstorming: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"brainstorm\", \"ideate\", \"generate ideas\", \"explore possibilities\", \"think creatively\", \"plan features\", \"diverge/converge\", or when a BHappy planner worker needs to facilitate interactive ideation sessions. Provides 36 proven techniques across 7 categories with research agent integration and decision pre-resolution.\n- code-reviewer:code-reviewer: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"review code\", \"check code quality\", \"code review\", \"security review\", \"find code issues\", \"check for bugs\", \"review pull request\", or when a BHappy QA worker needs to review code changes. Provides code review standards and security checks.\n- experiment-unifier:experiment-unifier: This skill should be used when consolidating parallel experiments, including \"unify experiments\", \"consolidate approaches\", \"merge duplicate efforts\", \"rationalize portfolio\", \"eliminate redundancy\", \"parallel experiments\", or when multiple projects are attempting to solve similar problems and need to be unified into a coherent approach. Integrates with BHappy's ExperimentAnalysis database model for tracking unification workflows.\n- feature-implementer:feature-implementer: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"implement a feature\", \"write code for feature\", \"TDD implementation\", \"test-driven development\", \"implement with tests first\", or when a BHappy developer worker needs to implement a feature following TDD methodology.\n- git-discipline:git-discipline: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"commit code\", \"create a branch\", \"manage git workflow\", \"atomic commits\", \"conventional commits\", or when performing any git operations. Provides commit hygiene, branch management, and git workflow standards for BHappy workers.\n- llm-manager:llm-manager\n- netrxn-devops:netrxn-devops: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"create ansible playbook\", \"configure docker compose\", \"set up caddy\", \"configure postgresql\", \"harden security\", \"set up backups\", \"check infrastructure status\", \"deploy to VM\", \"manage containers\", or needs guidance on Infrastructure-as-Code practices for homelab and Hetzner VMs using Ansible, Docker, Caddy, and PostgreSQL 17+.\n- nodejs-patterns:nodejs-patterns\n- python-patterns:python-patterns\n- security-patterns:security-patterns: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"implement authentication\", \"secure API endpoints\", \"handle secrets\", \"validate input\", \"prevent XSS\", \"prevent SQL injection\", \"review security\", \"OWASP compliance\", \"encrypt data\", \"manage sessions\", or needs guidance on application security patterns including authentication, authorization, input validation, secrets management, and OWASP Top 10 mitigations.\n- test-validator:test-validator: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"validate tests\", \"check test quality\", \"analyze test coverage\", \"review test suite\", \"test health check\", \"coverage analysis\", \"jest coverage\", \"go test coverage\", \"rust test coverage\", \"playwright test review\", \"e2e test validation\", or when a BHappy QA worker needs to assess test quality and coverage across any language. Provides multi-language test quality validation and coverage analysis.\n- test-writer:test-writer\n- claude-code-setup:claude-automation-recommender\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver: Audit and improve CLAUDE.md files in repositories. Use when user asks to check, audit, update, improve, or fix CLAUDE.md files. Scans for all CLAUDE.md files, evaluates quality against templates, outputs quality report, then makes targeted updates. Also use when the user mentions \"CLAUDE.md maintenance\" or \"project memory optimization\".\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Create distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces with high design quality. Use this skill when the user asks to build web components, pages, or applications. Generates creative, polished code that avoids generic AI aesthetics.\n- hookify:writing-rules\n- playground:playground: Creates interactive HTML playgrounds — self-contained single-file explorers that let users configure something visually through controls, see a live preview, and copy out a prompt. Use when the user asks to make a playground, explorer, or interactive tool for a topic.\n- plugin-dev:agent-development: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"create an agent\", \"add an agent\", \"write a subagent\", \"agent frontmatter\", \"when to use description\", \"agent examples\", \"agent tools\", \"agent colors\", \"autonomous agent\", or needs guidance on agent structure, system prompts, triggering conditions, or agent development best practices for Claude Code plugins.\n- plugin-dev:command-development: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"create a slash command\", \"add a command\", \"write a custom command\", \"define command arguments\", \"use command frontmatter\", \"organize commands\", \"create command with file references\", \"interactive command\", \"use AskUserQuestion in command\", or needs guidance on slash command structure, YAML frontmatter fields, dynamic arguments, bash execution in commands, user interaction patterns, or command development best practices for Claude Code.\n- plugin-dev:hook-development: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"create a hook\", \"add a PreToolUse/PostToolUse/Stop hook\", \"validate tool use\", \"implement prompt-based hooks\", \"use ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}\", \"set up event-driven automation\", \"block dangerous commands\", or mentions hook events (PreToolUse, PostToolUse, Stop, SubagentStop, SessionStart, SessionEnd, UserPromptSubmit, PreCompact, Notification). Provides comprehensive guidance for creating and implementing Claude Code plugin hooks with focus on advanced prompt-based hooks API.\n- plugin-dev:mcp-integration\n- plugin-dev:plugin-settings\n- plugin-dev:plugin-structure: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"create a plugin\", \"scaffold a plugin\", \"understand plugin structure\", \"organize plugin components\", \"set up plugin.json\", \"use ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}\", \"add commands/agents/skills/hooks\", \"configure auto-discovery\", or needs guidance on plugin directory layout, manifest configuration, component organization, file naming conventions, or Claude Code plugin architecture best practices.\n- plugin-dev:skill-development: This skill should be used when the user wants to \"create a skill\", \"add a skill to plugin\", \"write a new skill\", \"improve skill description\", \"organize skill content\", or needs guidance on skill structure, progressive disclosure, or skill development best practices for Claude Code plugins.\n- skill-creator:skill-creator\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- lean4:lean4: Use when editing .lean files, debugging Lean 4 builds (type mismatch, sorry, failed to synthesize instance, axiom warnings, lake build errors), searching mathlib for lemmas, formalizing mathematics in Lean, finding a counterexample to, refuting, or disproving a Lean statement, or learning Lean 4 concepts. Also trigger when the user asks for help with Lean 4, mathlib, or lakefile. Do NOT trigger for Coq/Rocq, Agda, Isabelle, HOL4, Mizar, Idris, Megalodon, or other non-Lean theorem provers.\n- portfolio-commercial-system:audit-commercial-release\n- portfolio-commercial-system:author-commercial-assets\n- portfolio-commercial-system:conduct-market-intelligence\n- portfolio-commercial-system:critique-commercial-assets\n- portfolio-commercial-system:develop-commercial-system\n- portfolio-commercial-system:onboard-commercial-instance\n- portfolio-commercial-system:orchestrate-commercial-work\n- portfolio-commercial-system:remediate-commercial-assets\n- skeft-qa:architecture-change: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"write an ADR\", \"write an architecture decision record\", \"spec this out\", \"review this spec\", \"review this ADR\", \"plan an infra build\", \"add a validation check\", \"add a gate\", \"add a ratchet\", \"wire in a new check\", \"add an agent, command, hook or skill to the plugin\", or \"change the architecture\" — and, unprompted, whenever a change would add or alter any part of SK_EFT_Hawking's own machinery (a check, a gate, an extractor, a node or edge type, a hook, a writer, a dashboard surface, a plugin component), or would create or edit anything under docs/adrs/, docs/architecture/, docs/superpowers/specs/, docs/superpowers/plans/, scripts/validation/checks/, or .claude/plugins/skeft-qa/. Invoke it BEFORE designing, and again before an ADR, spec or plan is treated as settled. For Lean proof work inside a /goal loop — the MCP proof loop, worktree fan-out, tactic friction — use goal-dev instead.\n- skeft-qa:goal-dev: In-loop development guidance for an autonomous /goal Lean-proof loop in SK_EFT_Hawking. This skill should be used when developing Lean 4 proofs inside a goal loop, deciding whether to fan work out to parallel worktree slots or stay solo, resetting a worktree slot, hitting a recurring Lean tactic/ elaboration friction, or sharding the lab notebook. It surfaces the MCP-first proof loop, kernel-purity rules, the worktree fan-out flow, and a symptom-indexed friction catalog. Invoke it at the start of a development stretch and whenever you hit proof-mechanics friction or need the parallel-dev recipe.\n- skeft-qa:harvest: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"run the harvest\", \"harvest the sessions\", \"extract process signal\", \"consolidate the register\", or when a scheduled task or second-session /loop fires the off-hot-loop harvest. It reads each managed /goal session's transcript from a watermark, extracts process/harness signal, and consolidates it into the System-2 register. Never invoke it inside a /goal session — it runs off the hot loop only.\n- skeft-qa:paper-authoring: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"draft a paper section\", \"write the bundle draft\", \"author the manuscript\", \"lift content into a bundle\", \"write up this wave for publication\", \"draft the abstract\", \"revise the paper prose\", or when editing any `papers/<bundle>/paper_draft.tex`. Provides the house voice, the prohibited-pattern floor, and venue conventions for SK-EFT Hawking publication bundles.\n- skeft-qa:sync: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"run sync\", \"sync the counts\", \"refresh the tables\", \"regenerate the deps\", \"update the citation cache\", or before a wave gate whenever counts, tables, or figures may have drifted from the Lean/Python sources. Runs the full mechanical Stage-12 sync (counts, tables, deps, citation cache) in one command; idempotent and regen-lock-serialized.\n- skeft-qa:wave-close\n- learn-anything:curriculum-architect: This skill should be used when the Learner Calibrator has produced the gap map and a learning plan needs designing. Applies 4C/ID whole-task instruction, Elaboration Theory epitome design, productive failure placement, and a seven-layer motivation architecture. Produces a sequenced curriculum with task classes, a dual-timeline schedule, assessment criteria, and plateau protocols. Includes a conversational checkpoint for epitome refinement with the learner. Output is structured JSON conforming to learning-plan.schema.json.\n- learn-anything:dashboard-generator\n- learn-anything:domain-assessor: This skill should be used when the user states a learning goal — any phrase like 'I want to learn X', 'teach me X', 'help me get better at X', or 'how do I learn X'. Classifies the skill type (motor/cognitive/perceptual/social), assesses the learning environment (kind vs. wicked), gathers the learner's background for transfer learning, and produces a constructive approach strategy. This is always the first step in the meta-learning pipeline. Output is structured JSON conforming to domain-assessment.schema.json.\n- learn-anything:learner-calibrator: This skill should be used when the Skill Researcher has produced a dependency graph and the learner's existing knowledge needs mapping. Walks through the graph conversationally, assessing mastery at key nodes using graph propagation and information-theoretic item selection to minimize questions needed. Produces the knowledge graph overlay (the 'gap map') that drives curriculum design. Output is structured JSON conforming to knowledge-graph.schema.json.\n- learn-anything:material-forge: This skill should be used when the Curriculum Architect has produced a learning plan and learning materials need generating, or on-demand when the Training Conductor needs new materials, or when the user invokes '/materials'. Orchestrates dedicated subagents to generate worked examples with fading, visual materials, SRS flashcard decks, assessment instruments, reference one-pagers, dependency graph visualizations, productive failure scenarios, interleaved practice sets, and encoding aids. All outputs conform to the system's JSON schemas and are exportable to Anki (.apkg), PDF, and Markdown.\n- learn-anything:orchestrator: The entry point and router for the meta-learning plugin system. Use this skill whenever a user wants to learn a new skill, continue a learning program, or interact with any part of the meta-learning system. It detects the current pipeline phase, routes to the appropriate component skill, manages state files, handles calibration loops, and ensures smooth handoffs between components. This skill should be triggered by any learning-related request: 'I want to learn X', 'let's do a training session', 'continue my learning', 'show me my progress', 'update my plan', or any reference to an ongoing learning engagement.\n- learn-anything:skill-researcher: This skill should be used when the user's learning goal has been classified by the Domain Assessor and needs deep investigation. Performs skill deconstruction into components, dependency graph construction, frequency/impact analysis, transfer pathway identification, failure point cataloging, and expert panel discovery. Uses web search extensively to ground decomposition in real expert perspectives. Output is a Skill Research Dossier conforming to skill-dossier.schema.json.\n- learn-anything:training-conductor: This skill should be used when a learner is ready for a training session — they've been through the assessment/research/calibration/curriculum pipeline and have a learning plan, or when the user invokes '/train'. Manages session flow (warm-up, deliberate practice, integration), adaptive teaching using Socratic questioning and the EMT escalation ladder, real-time difficulty calibration, in-session retrieval probes, mastery gate assessments, knowledge graph updates, external data integration (Anki, self-reports), plateau detection, motivation management, instructor persona adoption, and mentor conversation mode. Sessions are scoped to ~150k tokens. State is read at session start and written at session end.\n- anthropic-skills:canvas-design\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage\n- anthropic-skills:morning\n- anthropic-skills:pdf\n- anthropic-skills:pptx\n- anthropic-skills:schedule\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, handing the viewer a file to save, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: {"parentUuid":"a3174520-f460-42f5-9223-d93624d268ad","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce56GcY5y6XRTefKYmGU4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01GA71DPJynHh7YSrnS7ZwjL","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking/.claude/worktrees/redraft-D3 && grep -n -i \"frolov\\|fursaev\\|induced.gravity\\|1/4\" papers/D3/paper_draft.tex | head -40","description":"Grep Frolov-Fursaev claims"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4459,"cache_read_input_tokens":44901,"output_tokens":911,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":516},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4459},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":911,"cache_read_input_tokens":44901,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4459,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4459,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Ce56GbgjuDFPQw1uSStpH","attributionAgent":"Explore","attributionSkill":"skeft-qa:paper-authoring","attributionPlugin":"skeft-qa","type":"assistant","uuid":"2b917bd7-8aea-4d28-8d80-97c17e0e55e7","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:32:40.186Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
{"parentUuid":"a3174520-f460-42f5-9223-d93624d268ad","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UQ3boDRnKWSs16a78FdPKq","type":"tool_result","content":"\\section{Bekenstein--Hawking entropy from MTC state counting}\n\\label{sec:bh-entropy}\n\nThe substrate's microscopic entropy account ships a Kaul--Majumdar\nSU(2)$_k$ logarithmic correction with an explicitly negative\ncoefficient~\\cite{KaulMajumdar2000}, and an abelian-MTC falsifier that\nserves as a structural no-go on alternative microscopic theories.\n\n\\subsection{Closed form and Kaul--Majumdar coefficient}\n\nFor a Schwarzschild horizon of area $A$ in Planck units of the emergent\n$G_N$,\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:bh-entropy}\nS(A) \\;=\\; \\frac{A}{4G_N}\n- \\frac{3}{2}\\log\\!\\bigl(A/(4G_N)\\bigr) + O(1)\\,,\n\\end{equation}\n\\noindent\nwhere the $-3/2$ logarithmic coefficient\n(\\texttt{BHEntropyMicroscopic.kaul\\_majumdar\\_log\\_coefficient}) reads\noff the SU(2)$_k$ Chern--Simons partition function on the horizon. The\nprefactor matches the Verlinde formula at large area\n(\\texttt{verlinde\\_matches\\_kaul\\_majumdar\\_at\\_large\\_area}) and decomposes as\n$-3/2 = -1/2$ (Gaussian saddle) $-1$ (logarithmic Verlinde-formula\nmodular-transform piece)\n(\\texttt{kaul\\_majumdar\\_log\\_decomposition}). The $1/4$ in $A/(4G_N)$ is, in the\nLQG/Verlinde-counting route of this section, a horizon boundary-condition (Immirzi)\ntuning; in the ADW Sakharov induced-gravity route it is derived Immirzi-independently\n(\\texttt{InducedGravityEntropy.frolov\\_fursaev\\_quarter\\_coefficient}, Wave~9): the\nsame Seeley--DeWitt $a_2$ that induces $G_N$ also fixes the horizon entanglement-entropy\narea density, with the heat-kernel-to-Einstein--Hilbert ratio structurally $4$ (FFZ,\nhep-th/9607104). The $-3/2$ log is genuinely derived from the literal SU(2) singlet count:\n\\texttt{verlindeEntropy\\_SU2k} is now (Wave~7C) the \\emph{faithful} $\\Gamma$-Catalan\nlog-dimension $\\log\\!\\bigl[\\Gamma(2x{+}1)/(\\Gamma(x{+}1)\\Gamma(x{+}2))\\bigr]$ at\n$x = A/(8 G_N \\log 2)$, a genuine analytic object, not the earlier saddle self-definition\n(closing substrate-audit \\#13), and the Kaul--Majumdar closed form is a \\emph{derived\ncorollary}: \\texttt{gaussianSaddleAsymptotic} is the genuine $O(1/A)$ rate\n$\\bigl|\\texttt{verlindeEntropy\\_SU2k}(A,G_N) - \\texttt{kaulMajumdarS}(A,G_N,\\kappa_{\\mathrm{KM}})\\bigr|\n= O(1/A)$ with the literal constant $\\kappa_{\\mathrm{KM}} = \\tfrac{3}{2}\\log(2\\log 2) -\n\\tfrac{1}{2}\\log\\pi$. The discrete singlet count carries the sharp $O(1/m)$ rate\n(\\texttt{LaplaceMethodAsymptotic.log\\_singletCount\\_sub\\_rate}); the continuum $O(1/A)$ rate\nrests on a \\texttt{Real.Gamma} Stirling-with-remainder built kernel-pure from Mathlib primitives\nvia a Bohr--Mollerup convexity squeeze\n(\\texttt{GammaStirling.logGamma\\_sub\\_stirlingPart\\_isBigO}, previously absent from Mathlib),\nwith no Hardy--Ramanujan input. The two routes are consistent\n(\\texttt{frolov\\_fursaev\\_matches\\_kaulMajumdar\\_leading}).\n\n\\subsection{Five falsifier theorems for the boundary-condition bundle}\n\nThe horizon-MTC boundary-condition tracked-hypothesis bundle carries\nfive falsifier theorems\n(\\texttt{H\\_HorizonBoundaryCondition\\_falsifier\\_positivity},\n\\texttt{\\_falsifier\\_logBound},\n\\texttt{\\_falsifier\\_secondLaw},\n\\texttt{\\_falsifier\\_quarterCoefficient},\nand an envelope\n\\texttt{H\\_HorizonBoundaryCondition\\_implies\\_nonabelian\\_envelope}). Each\nfalsifier is a constructive obstruction.\n\n\\subsection{Witt-invariant sharpening of the anomaly-matching condition}\n\nThe bundle's anomaly-matching consistency field is sharpened beyond its perturbative form\n(Wave~8B). The horizon chiral central charge $c_- = 8 N_f$ satisfies the elementary mod-8\ncondition $8 \\mid c_-$ for \\emph{every} $N_f$\n(\\texttt{HorizonWittBoundary.horizon\\_wave8\\_anomalyMatch\\_always}), so on its own it does not\nconstrain the ADW model. The genuine, non-vacuous consistency condition is Witt-triviality of the\nboundary, $24 \\mid c_-$, which holds \\emph{if and only if $3 \\mid N_f$}\n(\\texttt{horizon\\_wittTrivial\\_iff\\_three\\_generations}; unconditional, wiring the SymTFT\nWitt-class machinery). The perturbative $8 \\mid c_-$ is exactly its necessary shadow\n(\\texttt{wittTrivial\\_implies\\_mod8}), and the gap is strict: $N_f = 1$ passes $8 \\mid c_-$ but\nfails $24 \\mid c_-$ (\\texttt{horizon\\_anomalyMatch\\_strictly\\_weaker\\_than\\_witt}). This ties the\nhorizon boundary condition to the program's three-generation result through the \\emph{same} Witt\ninvariant. The physical reading, that Witt-triviality is equivalent to the boundary admitting a gapped\n(Lagrangian-algebra) topological boundary, is the Davydov--M\\\"uger--Nikshych--Ostrik (2010, arXiv:1009.2117)\ncorrespondence, of which the perturbative $8 \\mid c_-$ is only the necessary part.\n\n\\subsection{Abelian-MTC falsifier}\n\n\\texttt{abelian\\_MTC\\_falsifies\\_H\\_HorizonBoundaryCondition}: an abelian\nhorizon MTC cannot reproduce the Kaul--Majumdar $-3/2$ logarithmic\ncoefficient on the same area. This is a closure on the substrate's\nmicroscopic-MTC choice: if one tries to substitute the SU(2)$_k$\nChern--Simons partition with an abelian alternative, the Bekenstein--Hawking\nagreement fails. The Sen 4D Schwarzschild log-coefficient\n$+(212/45-3)=77/45\\approx+1.71$~\\cite{Sen2013} \\emph{disagrees} with the\nKaul--Majumdar $-3/2$\n(\\texttt{sen\\_4d\\_disagrees\\_with\\_kaul\\_majumdar},\n\\texttt{sen\\_4d\\_quantitative\\_disagreement\\_bound}), an independent\nno-go on the alternative-MTC channel.\n\n\\subsection{Same $G_N$ across BCH register}\n\nThe leading $A/(4G_N)$ in~\\eqref{eq:bh-entropy} consumes the same\n$G_N^{\\mathrm{emerg}}$ produced in \\S\\ref{sec:linearized-efe}; the\nexplicit match is\n\\texttt{kaul\\_majumdar\\_leading\\_matches\\_G\\_N\\_emerg}. The same\n$G_N^{\\mathrm{emerg}}$ then enters the regime-partition mass\n$M_c$ of \\S\\ref{sec:bch-four-laws}.\n\n\\subsection{QEC cross-bridge anchor (forward to \\S\\ref{sec:qec-bridge})}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"1cd88c92-45b5-465f-8f8a-ea40114f92e9","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:32:40.278Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a3174520-f460-42f5-9223-d93624d268ad","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"3bfd265d-f4f1-410f-86b9-33cced63d77c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CnsQRrQbuP8ENoej3f1JZU","type":"tool_result","content":"Here the vector sign on J~ stands not for all components of the angular momentum but only the Cartan\ngenerators. Thus for example for the SO(3) rotation group J~ is a one component vector labelling J3 .\n2\n\n6\n\n\fFinally we can consider the entropy in the ensemble containing only singlet states of the\n~\nrotation group. If we denote the corresponding entropy by Ssinglet then exp[Ssinglet (M, Q)]δM\n~\ncounts the total number of rotationally invariant states in the mass interval δM and charge Q.\n~ takes the form:\nOur result for Ssinglet (M, Q)\n\u0012\n\u0013\n1\n1\n1\n~\n~\n~\nSsinglet (M, Q) = SBH (M, J = 0, Q) + ln a Clocal − (D − 4) − (D − 2)NR − (D − 4)nV ,\n2\n2\n2\n(1.5)\nwhere NR = (D − 1)(D − 2)/2 is the total number of generators of the rotation group.\nIn a theory of pure gravity in D = 4, (1.2) gives Clocal = 212/45. Also we have NR = 3.\n\u0001\nHence the logarithmic correction to Ssinglet is given by ∆Ssinglet = 212\n−\n3\nln a. In contrast we\n45\n\nfind that loop quantum gravity result of [29–41] predicts a result of −2 ln a.3 The two results\nobviously disagree.\n\nSince on the macroscopic side the computation is based on one loop determinant of masless\nfields in the black hole background, one could ask whether higher loops can give additional\nlogarithmic corrections to the black hole entropy. We have checked, based on naive power\ncounting arguments, that higher loops do not give any logarithmic corrections to the entropy.\nThis is related to the fact that in a theory of gravity infrared divergences become softer at higher\nloops. It has also been argued in [18] that neither massive fields nor higher derivative corrections\nto the action can affect the logarithmic correction to the entropy. Thus the macroscopic results\nfor logarithmic corrections seem quite robust.\nThe rest of the paper is organized as follows. In §2 we describe the computation of logarithmic corrections to the grand canonical partition functions using Euclidean gravity path\nintegral. In §3 we discuss how to translate these results into a statement of logarithmic corrections to the black hole entropy in different ensembles. In §4 we compare the macroscopic\n\nprediction for the logarithmic correction to the entropy of a Schwarzschild black hole to the\nprediction of loop quantum gravity. Finally in §5 we review how euclidean gravity approach\ncan be used to correctly reproduce the logarithmic corrections to the entropy of a BTZ black\nhole. Appendix A contains an analysis of the zero modes of the euclidean black hole solution,\n\nand in appendix B we describe the procedure for removing from the partition function the\ncontribution due to the thermal gas around the black hole.\nFor example the result of [30] gives a logarithmic correction of − 23 ln AH = −3 ln a for the entropy of singlet\nstates of the rotation group. But this counts the number of states per unit interval in the area variable AH .\nConverting this to number of states per unit interval in mass gives a result of −2 ln a.\n3","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"cfd4dd30-a6fb-4228-8e9f-73174e176358","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:33:22.295Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3bfd265d-f4f1-410f-86b9-33cced63d77c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
{"parentUuid":"6871bb59-a880-493a-b6d7-d954ea9150c1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C5ZrwG9AfcjSV2PCYPZg6i","type":"tool_result","content":"E-mail: sen@mri.ernet.in\n\nAbstract\nEuclidean gravity method has been successful in computing logarithmic corrections to extremal black hole entropy in terms of low energy data, and gives results in perfect agreement\nwith the microscopic results in string theory. Motivated by this success we apply Euclidean\ngravity to compute logarithmic corrections to the entropy of various non-extremal black holes\nin different dimensions, taking special care of integration over the zero modes and keeping track\nof the ensemble in which the computation is done. These results provide strong constraint on\nany ultraviolet completion of the theory if the latter is able to give an independent computation\nof the entropy of non-extremal black holes from microscopic description. For Schwarzschild\nblack holes in four space-time dimensions the macroscopic result seems to disagree with the\nexisting result in loop quantum gravity.\n\n1\n\n\fContents\n1 Introduction and summary\n\n2\n\n2 Grand canonical partition function\n2.1 General framework . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\n\n8\n8\n\n2.2\n2.3\n\nHeat kernel . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\nOne loop contribution to the partition function from the non-zero modes . . .","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"15acb743-d963-4e6c-a2ab-c0bc2476e241","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:33:24.037Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6871bb59-a880-493a-b6d7-d954ea9150c1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

The Einstein--Cartan torsion\nprediction $|T_{\\mathrm{EC}}| \\simeq 2.05\\times 10^{-77}\\,\\mathrm{GeV}$\nclears the Kostelecky--Russell--Tasson cosmic-axial-torsion\nbound~\\cite{KosteleckyRussellTasson2008} of $10^{-31}\\,\\mathrm{GeV}$ by\nroughly forty-six orders of magnitude\n(\\texttt{EinsteinCartanExtension.torsionAtCosmologicalBackground\\_at\\_planck\\_natural\\_below\\_kostelecky}).\n\n\\paragraph{What \\emph{reproduces} the open problem.}\nThe cosmological constant inherits the heat-kernel $a_0$ coefficient. At\nthe same natural point $(\\Lambda_{\\mathrm{UV}},N_f) = (M_{\\mathrm{Pl}},16)$\nthe prediction overshoots the Planck-2018 observed\n$\\Lambda_{\\mathrm{obs}} \\simeq 2.6\\times 10^{-47}\\,\\mathrm{GeV}^{4}$ by\n$\\gtrsim 10^{100}$ (heuristically $\\sim 10^{122}$)\n(\\texttt{MicroscopicCoefficientMatch.lambdaEmergMicroscopic\\_at\\_planck\\_natural\\_far\\_exceeds\\_observed}).\nThis is the classical Weinberg cosmological-constant\nproblem~\\cite{Weinberg1989} reproduced, not solved, inside the\nemergent-gravity formulation. We report this as a first-class\nresult. Induced-gravity literature from Sakharov to\nAdler~\\cite{Sakharov1968,Adler1980} left open whether emergent\ngravity \\emph{shelters} from the cosmological-constant problem. The\ncalculation answers no.\n\n\\paragraph{Microscopic flesh.}\nSix rungs of internal physics make the substrate quantitative rather than\nschematic. The Wetterich--NJL scalar channel identifies the Higgs sector\n(\\S\\ref{sec:scalar-rung}); the Majorana rung's type-I seesaw with a\n$\\mathbb{Z}_{16}$ singlet bridge anchors the lepton sector\n(\\S\\ref{sec:majorana-rung}); the electroweak phase transition is a\ncrossover at the physical Higgs mass~\\cite{KLRS1996}, so the substrate's\nelectroweak-baryogenesis channel is excluded by Sakharov's third condition\non \\emph{independent} grounds (\\S\\ref{sec:ewpt}--\\ref{sec:ewbg-bridge}); the\nPolyakov-loop centre symmetry $\\mathbb{Z}_N$ confines deconfinement on the\nexpected Svetitsky--Yaffe universality class (\\S\\ref{sec:confinement}); the\nGell-Mann--Oakes--Renner relation~\\cite{GellMannOakesRenner1968} closes\nchiral symmetry breaking with a PDG-anchored numerical\nmatch (\\S\\ref{sec:chiral-ssb}); and the CFL chiral\nLagrangian~\\cite{AlfordRajagopalWilczek1999} ships with a machine-checked\nformalisation of emergent-$\\mathbb{Z}_3$ matching the QCD centre\n(\\S\\ref{sec:cfl}).\n\n\\paragraph{Algebraic-Lorentzian-geometry ground floor.}\npresent-day epoch),\n\\begin{equation}\n|T_{\\mathrm{EC}}| \\simeq 2.05\\times 10^{-77}\\,\\mathrm{GeV}\\,.\n\\end{equation}\n\\noindent\nLean theorem \\texttt{EinsteinCartanExtension.torsionAmplitude\\_pos}\ncloses positivity;\n\\texttt{torsionAtCosmologicalBackground\\_pos\\_at\\_natural\\_params} closes\nthe natural-point evaluation; the\nmatch-residual biconditional\n\\texttt{ecResidual\\_eq\\_zero\\_iff\\_alpha\\_unity} closes\n$\\delta T_{\\mathrm{EC}} = 0$ if and only if $\\alpha_{\\mathrm{EC}} = 1$.\n\n\\subsection{Bound-passage closure}\n\nThe Kostelecky--Russell--Tasson cosmic-axial-torsion bound caps\n$|T_{\\mathrm{EC}}| \\le 10^{-31}\\,\\mathrm{GeV}$. The substrate's\nprediction sits forty-six orders of magnitude beneath, closed in\n\\texttt{EinsteinCartanExtension.torsionAtCosmologicalBackground\\_at\\_planck\\_natural\\_below\\_kostelecky}.\nThe (older, less-stringent) Hughes--Drever isotropy\nbound~\\cite{HughesDrever1960} is implied\n(\\texttt{below\\_kostelecky\\_implies\\_below\\_hughes\\_drever}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-channel chained bound: torsionBound chain}\n\nThe substrate's torsion register chains: Hughes--Drever 1960 to\nKostelecky--Russell--Tasson 2008 to the \\emph{substrate prediction at natural\npoint} to laboratory measurement at low energies. Each link is a Lean\nbiconditional closure;\n\\texttt{torsionBoundKostelecky\\_lt\\_hughesDrever} certifies the\nordering.\n\n\\subsection{Discussion: torsion as a soft observational signature}\n\nThe substrate's torsion amplitude is small but non-zero; the substrate\npredicts a soft torsion signature that current experiments do not\ndetect but future axial-torsion-sensitive measurements may. {"parentUuid":"ea2a8153-aa32-412d-8d53-41370de02f35","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JWSnWecGkqvtNugswqrTVJ","type":"tool_result","content":"The work consolidated here treats emergent gravity not as one topic but as\nthe single coherent face of a substrate whose predictive content is fixed by\na heat-kernel calibration. The substrate is the antisymmetric-tetrad\nbilinear of Akama, Diakonov, and Wetterich (ADW), augmented by the\nWetterich--Hubbard--Stratonovich apparatus that drives a tetrad gap\nequation~\\cite{Akama1978,Diakonov2011,Wetterich2017}. The calibration is\nVassilevich's Christensen--Duff coefficient~\\cite{Vassilevich2003,\nChristensenDuff1978} extracted from a Dirac heat-kernel expansion at order\n$a_2$, which delivers a Sakharov--Adler closed\nform~\\cite{Sakharov1968,Adler1980} for Newton's constant,\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:sakharov-identity}\nG_N^{\\mathrm{Sak}}(\\Lambda_{\\mathrm{UV}},\\,N_f) \\;=\\;\n\\frac{12\\pi}{N_f\\,\\Lambda_{\\mathrm{UV}}^{2}}\\,,\n\\end{equation}\n\\S\\ref{sec:linearized-efe}--\\S\\ref{sec:einstein-cartan}: without a\nQCD-side voucher, the gravity claims rest on an unvalidated substrate.\n\n\n%% =====================================================================\n%% §17 — Heat-kernel calibration + Sakharov-Adler ↔ G_N (Decision Gate E.2)\n%% Lift: paper39_heat_kernel_expansion\n%% =====================================================================\n\\section{Emergent-gravity calibration through $a_4$}\n\\label{sec:heat-kernel}\n\nThe Christensen--Duff Dirac heat-kernel\nexpansion~\\cite{ChristensenDuff1978,Vassilevich2003} on the substrate\ngenerates the calibration coefficients $a_0, a_2, a_4$ that close the\nsingle-coefficient identity~\\eqref{eq:sakharov-identity}.\n\n\\subsection{Heat-kernel asymptotic and Dirac coefficients}\n\nFor a Dirac operator on a $4$-dimensional Riemannian manifold,\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:heat-kernel-asymptotic}\n\\mathrm{Tr}\\,e^{-\\tau D^2} \\;\\sim\\; \\frac{1}{(4\\pi)^{2}\\tau^{2}}\n\\bigl( a_0 + a_2\\,\\tau + a_4\\,\\tau^{2} + \\ldots \\bigr)\\,,\n\\qquad \\tau \\to 0^+,\n\\end{equation}\n\\noindent\nwith $a_0(N_f) = N_f$ (one species per Dirac fermion;\n\\texttt{HeatKernelExpansion.a0\\_dirac\\_pos},\n\\texttt{a0\\_dirac\\_linear}); $a_2$ proportional to $-N_f \\,R/12$\nthrough the Christensen--Duff prefactor (\\texttt{a2\\_R\\_coefficient\\_neg},\n\\texttt{a2\\_R\\_coefficient\\_linear}); and $a_4$ a sum of\nholds at one-loop on the Dirac sector. Multi-loop corrections to $G_N$\nare tracked-hypothesis claims; the open one-loop calculation\nof \\S\\ref{sec:linearized-efe} is the bottleneck.\n\n\n\\subsection{Sakharov--horizon-Crooks unification: the four-condition criterion as fluctuation-theorem content (Phase 6n.\\(\\zeta\\))}\n\\label{sec:sakharov-horizon-crooks}\n\nThe Sakharov--Adler closed form of \\S\\ref{sec:heat-kernel} is the\n\\emph{equilibrium} projection of a deeper \\emph{non-equilibrium}\nfluctuation-theorem statement. Specifically, the Volovik--Jannes\nfour-condition Sakharov criterion~\\cite{JacobsonVolovik1998,Volovik2003BraneBH}\nformalised in the program's\n\\texttt{JacobsonThermoGRDarkEnergy.lean} as\n\\texttt{SakharovConditions} (with theorems \\texttt{JTGR6}--\\texttt{JTGR9}\nidentifying the witness substrates) is conjectured biconditional, at\nthe Stage-1 substrate-level encoded in\n\\texttt{lean/SKEFTHawking/CrooksAnalogHawking/SakharovHorizonCrooks.lean}\n(the explicit \\texttt{Sakharov\\_iff\\_horizon\\_Crooks} bridge is itself\ndeferred to Stage 2--3 of the same module). Under that conjectured\nbiconditional, the Sakharov-Adler closed-form result of\n\\S\\ref{sec:heat-kernel} \\emph{can be read as} the equilibrium\nprojection of a deeper non-equilibrium fluctuation-theorem statement\nrather than a stand-alone identity. The Stage-1 statement is the\nhorizon-Crooks detailed-balance identity\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:horizon-crooks}\n\\frac{P_{\\mathrm{F}}[\\gamma]}{P_{\\mathrm{R}}[\\bar\\gamma]} \\;=\\;\n\\exp\\!\\bigl(\\sigma[\\gamma]\\bigr)\n\\end{equation}\n\\noindent\nof Crooks~\\cite{Crooks1999}, specialised to the local Rindler horizon\nof the substrate-induced gravity. Here $\\sigma[\\gamma]$ is the entropy\nproduction along the substrate trajectory $\\gamma$ and $\\bar\\gamma$ is\nits time-reversed image under the Sakharov-induced emergent SO(3,1)\naction. Equation~\\eqref{eq:horizon-crooks} is the trajectory-form","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"ac7f98b7-e919-4941-bd70-1be3cb83f59f","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:34:06.518Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ea2a8153-aa32-412d-8d53-41370de02f35","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}
{"parentUuid":"d8b0b5bb-e42e-4fa1-bb4d-1f767d1d6efe","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Fw4r9eP2g9rmC7haMJ6o3n","type":"tool_result","content":"\\figuredeferred{fig:d3-adw-bifurcation}{the tetrad-gap bifurcation diagram; \\texttt{adw/} solves the gap equation but the bifurcation sweep is a separate production run that has not been made}\n\\section{Gauge erasure: only $U(1)$ survives the fluid layer}\n\\label{sec:gauge-erasure}\n\nA higher-form-symmetry analysis of Glorioso--Liu Schwinger--Keldysh effective\nfield theory~\\cite{GloriosoLiu2018} establishes that the hydrodynamic layer\n\\emph{erases} non-Abelian one-form gauge symmetry: only $U(1)$ survives the\nfluid-cell coarse-graining as a continuous one-form, with non-Abelian\ngauge fields surviving only as discrete centre symmetries that re-enter\nthrough the deconfinement transition (\\S\\ref{sec:confinement}). The erasure\ntheorem (Lean: \\texttt{GaugeErasure.gauge\\_erasure} +\n\\texttt{GaugeErasure.u1\\_survival}) is the\n\\emph{first} of three predictive-boundary cuts that force the ADW route\nto the tetrad-condensation channel.\n\n\\subsection{Erasure mechanism}\n\nNon-Abelian one-form symmetries require a colour-charged operator algebra\non each fluid cell. Hydrodynamic coarse-graining washes out colour\ncorrelation outside the screening length while preserving Abelian charge,\nSchwarzschild branch.\n\n\\subsection{Hawking-temperature profiles per regime}\n\nIn the Schwarzschild branch,\n$T_H = 1/(8\\pi G_N^{\\mathrm{emerg}} M)$ is strictly decreasing in\n$M$~\\cite{Hawking1975}\n(\\texttt{T\\_H\\_schwarzschild\\_strict\\_decreasing}); in the ADW-extremality\nbranch the cooling sign is anchored to the sonic-hole backreaction of\nBalbinot \\textit{et al.}~\\cite{Balbinot2005}, whose emission temperature\ndecreases as the hole radiates, against the Jacobson--Koike thin-film\ncontrast~\\cite{JacobsonKoike2002}, whose\n$T_H(v) = T_H(0)\\,(1 - v^2/c_\\perp^2)$ rises to a non-vanishing end\ntemperature as radiation slows the wall; the substrate carries an\nexplicit cooling evolution\n$T_H(t) = T_{H0}\\,e^{-t/\\tau_{\\mathrm{cool}}}$\n(\\texttt{T\\_H\\_acoustic\\_evolution\\_strict\\_decreasing}). The two\nprofiles are classified by an inductive\n\\texttt{Regime}-type discriminant at $M = M_c$ (discrete classification\nin the Lean substrate; the splash-companion L3 makes this discrete\ncharacter explicit). The substrate carries no continuity-biconditional\ngluing the two branches into a single smooth profile; the regime\npartition is a hard classification boundary.\n\n\\subsection{Four laws in two regimes}\n\nThe BCH four laws~\\cite{BardeenCarterHawking1973} read on the substrate as:\n\\textbf{Zeroth.} Surface gravity $\\kappa$ is constant on the horizon in\nboth regimes. \\textbf{First.} $dM = (\\kappa/8\\pi G_N)\\,dA + \\Omega\\,dJ +\n\\Phi\\,dQ$ holds in both regimes, with the substrate $G_N^{\\mathrm{emerg}}$\non the right-hand side. \\textbf{Second.} $dA/dt \\ge 0$ via the\nGlorioso--Liu Schwinger--Keldysh entropy-current\nmonotonicity~\\cite{GloriosoLiu2018}, \\emph{without invoking a pointwise\nNull Energy Condition}; this is the substrate's entropy-current upgrade\nrelative to classical Hawking 1971. \\textbf{Third.} The Israel\nstrong-form is preserved in the Schottky-natural Schwarzschild\nbranch~\\cite{Israel1986}; the Kehle--Unger\nviolation~\\cite{KehleUnger2024} is admitted in BPS-violating matter\nsectors at the ADW-extremality limit, but only as a structural admission,\nnot a closed Lean theorem. The five\nfalsifier theorems\n\\texttt{falsifier\\_acoustic\\_decay\\_form},\n\\texttt{falsifier\\_schwarzschild\\_heating},\n\\texttt{falsifier\\_third\\_law\\_form},\n\\texttt{falsifier\\_alpha\\_ADW\\_dependence}, and\n\\texttt{four\\_laws\\_consistent\\_with\\_acoustic\\_regime} certify the\nregister at the Lean level.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-bundle anchor to L3}\n\n\\eqref{eq:Mc-regime} and the regime-partition criterion appear identically","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"d95c347d-9c0b-4a6e-8443-49b7b78bde9e","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:34:06.525Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d8b0b5bb-e42e-4fa1-bb4d-1f767d1d6efe","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

One can easily adapt (2.12)\n999:\fconnection to the standard equilibrium entropy we should identify the new entropy current\n--- (empty above = term absent) ---\n##### second law statement\nMIT-CTP/4859\n\nFebruary 20, 2017\n\nThe second law of thermodynamics from symmetry and unitarity\nPaolo Glorioso1 and Hong Liu2\n1\n\nKadanoff Center for Theoretical Physics,\n\narXiv:1612.07705v2 [hep-th] 17 Feb 2017\n\nUniversity of Chicago, Chicago, IL 60637, USA\n2\n\nCenter for Theoretical Physics,\n\nMassachusetts Institute of Technology, Cambridge, MA 02139\n\nAbstract\nThe second law of thermodynamics states that for a thermally isolated system entropy never decreases. Most physical processes we observe in nature involve variations of macroscopic quantities\nover spatial and temporal scales much larger than microscopic molecular collision scales and thus\ncan be considered as in local equilibrium. For a many-body system in local equilibrium a stronger\nversion of the second law applies which says that the entropy production at each spacetime point\nshould be non-negative. In this paper we provide a proof of the second law for such systems and\na first derivation of the local second law. For this purpose we develop a general non-equilibrium\neffective field theory of slow degrees of freedom from integrating out fast degrees of freedom in a\nquantum many-body system and consider its classical limit. The key elements of the proof are the\npresence of a Z2 symmetry, which can be considered as a proxy for local equilibrium and microtime-reversibility, and a classical remnant of quantum unitarity. The Z2 symmetry leads to a local\ncurrent from a procedure analogous to that used in the Noether theorem. Unitarity leads to a\ndefinite sign of the divergence of the current. We also discuss the origin of an arrow of time, as\nwell as the coincidence of causal and thermodynamical arrows of time. Applied to hydrodynamics,\nthe proof gives a first-principle derivation of the phenomenological entropy current condition and\nprovides a constructive procedure for obtaining the entropy current.\n\n1\n\n\fI.\n\nINTRODUCTION","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"99356d58-5f26-4158-91d8-2c4717630ad0","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:34:24.234Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c8c647d7-3f24-4a62-959d-6433a077f26b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

Alternatively, one can use\nthe analysis of Christensen and Duff [127] who calculated a4 for arbitrary spin\nfields (see also [69]). The results are collected in Table 1. Some comments are in\norder. Spin 1/2 means 4-component Dirac spinors. For spin 1 and spin 2 fields\nwe took into account contributions from corresponding ghost fields. Note, that\nvector Yang-Mills fields and vector ghosts for gravity interact differently with\nthe background geometry. The cosmological constant is taken to be zero.\nAs a physical application of the heat kernel expansion in curved space we\nmay mention, for example, the asymptotic conformal invariance phenomenon\nwhich was studied by using this technique in [99, 103, 104, 108].\nA similar analysis can be performed also in the presence of boundaries\n[333].\n4.3 DeWitt iterative procedure\nThe iterative method by DeWitt uses separation of the heat kernel with\nnon-coinciding arguments into a leading part (which is non-analytic in t) and\nthe power-law corrections. For a flat manifold this separation has been described in sec. 1 (cf. eqs. (1.12) and (1.13)). Let us consider a massless scalar\nfield on a curved compact Riemannian manifold M. {"parentUuid":"8cfaecee-01ff-4744-9a55-ab399b7d21a0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01A6kNivxrbtZwGhaWYycbNE","type":"tool_result","content":"%% =====================================================================\n\\subsection{Chiral spontaneous symmetry breaking: Gell-Mann--Oakes--Renner}\n\\label{sec:chiral-ssb}\n\nThe Gell-Mann--Oakes--Renner relation~\\cite{GellMannOakesRenner1968}\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:gmor}\nf_\\pi^2\\,m_\\pi^2 \\;=\\; -2\\,m_q\\,\\langle\\bar{q}q\\rangle\n\\end{equation}\n\\noindent\ncloses the chiral-SSB content of the substrate against the Particle\nData Group~\\cite{PDG2024}.\n\n\\subsection{Quark condensate and structural negativity}\n\nThe quark condensate $\\langle\\bar{q}q\\rangle$ is structurally negative;\n\\texttt{ChiralSSB\\_QCD.not\\_isQuarkCondensateCandidate\\_of\\_too\\_negative}\ncertifies the negativity-of-the-vacuum structure. The substrate's\nWetterich--NJL apparatus delivers the same negativity from the same\nfour-fermion contact channel that drove the gap equation of\n\\S\\ref{sec:adw-gap}; one mechanism, two outputs.\n\n\\subsection{GMOR PDG match}\n\n\\texttt{ChiralSSB\\_QCD.gmor\\_pdg\\_match} discharges the numerical match\nof \\eqref{eq:gmor} against FLAG-2021 central values at the precision\nthe named theorem encodes: $f_\\pi = 92\\,\\mathrm{MeV}$,\n$m_\\pi = 137\\,\\mathrm{MeV}$, $m_q = 3.5\\,\\mathrm{MeV}$ (mean of\n$m_u + m_d$), and $\\langle\\bar{q}q\\rangle = -0.0227\\,\\mathrm{GeV}^3 =\n-2.27\\times 10^7\\,\\mathrm{MeV}^3 \\approx -(283\\,\\mathrm{MeV})^3$;\nboth sides of \\eqref{eq:gmor} evaluate to $1.589\\times 10^{-4}\\,\n\\mathrm{GeV}^4$ within the\n$10^{-4}$ GMOR tolerance. The more precise PDG~2024 central values\n$f_\\pi = 92.07\\,\\mathrm{MeV}$, $m_\\pi = 134.98\\,\\mathrm{MeV}$,\n$m_q = 3.45\\,\\mathrm{MeV}$ lie within the same GMOR tolerance band\nbut are not the values discharged by the named theorem.\n\n\\subsection{Chiral-unbroken phase ruled out}\n\n\\texttt{ChiralSSB\\_QCD.chiral\\_unbroken\\_violates\\_gmor} closes the\ncontrapositive: a chiral-symmetric vacuum with $\\langle\\bar{q}q\\rangle = 0$\nviolates GMOR for any $m_q > 0$, and is therefore excluded by PDG. This\nis a structural no-go on the alternative chiral-symmetric channel for\nthe substrate's QCD sector.\n\n\\subsection{Tetrad-VEV / quark-condensate scale naturalness}\n\n\\texttt{H\\_TetradQuarkScalesNatural} is a tracked-hypothesis bundle\nasserting that the substrate's tetrad VEV scale and the QCD quark\ncondensate scale are within the natural range","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"00ec0317-8f47-479e-ac9c-c2b133586731","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:34:48.811Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8cfaecee-01ff-4744-9a55-ab399b7d21a0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"e81e4c05-c188-454d-b64f-3df3d58c15d1","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WEcyGaGsHp9u33kcMcmgmW","type":"tool_result","content":"The summarizes much of particle physics and cosmology. Using data from previous editions, plus 2,717 new measurements from 869 papers, we list, evaluate, and average measured properties of gauge bosons and the recently discovered Higgs boson, leptons, quarks, mesons, and baryons. We summarize searches for hypothetical particles such as supersymmetric particles, heavy bosons, axions, dark photons, etc. Particle properties and search limits are listed in Summary Tables. We give numerous tables, figures, formulae, and reviews of topics such as Higgs Boson Physics, Supersymmetry, Grand Unified Theories, Neutrino Mixing, Dark Energy, Dark Matter, Cosmology, Particle Detectors, Colliders, Probability and Statistics. Most of the 120 reviews are updated, including many that are heavily revised. The is divided into two volumes. Volume 1 includes the Summary Tables and 97 review articles. Volume 2 consists of the Particle Listings and contains also 23 reviews that address specific aspects of the data presented in the Listings. The complete (both volumes) is published online on the website of the Particle Data Group () and in a journal. Volume 1 is available in print as the . A with the Summary Tables and essential tables, figures, and equations from selected review articles is available in print, as a web version optimized for use on phones, and as an Android app. The 2024 edition of the Review of Particle Physics should be cited as: S. {"parentUuid":"b1b6e512-dfc5-45a7-9541-0cd7f605230a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce56WJCQntiwi4Ub1UpuV","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I'm looking at the cosmological constant overshoot claim around lines 1782-1785, where the text says the substrate's prediction exceeds the observed value by over $10^{100}$, and notes a residual two-orders gap between the Lean-verified lower bound and the heuristic estimate — I want to check whether that gap is actually justified or just asserted.\n\nActually this doesn't add up: the heuristic estimate elsewhere in the document is ~$10^{122}$, not $10^{100}$, so calling $10^{100}$ the heuristic and describing only a \"two-orders gap\" to it is inconsistent — the real gap between $10^{100}$ and $10^{122}$ is 22 orders of magnitude, not two. This looks like a genuine discrepancy: line 1785 mislabels which number is rigorous versus heuristic and understates the gap, contradicting the consistent $10^{122}$ figure used elsewhere (lines 51-53, 197, 2351). {"parentUuid":"f22e35d5-f74f-4551-9c3e-79ad678f1b66","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"ae24566403217eb9b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016X345Vy2wyN8iZhyMTMZQo","type":"tool_result","content":"### KehleUnger2024.pdf:\nExtremal black hole formation as a critical phenomenon\nChristoph Kehle∗1 and Ryan Unger†2\n1\n\nInstitute for Theoretical Studies & Department of Mathematics, ETH Zürich,\nClausiusstrasse 47, 8092 Zürich, Switzerland\n\narXiv:2402.10190v1 [gr-qc] 15 Feb 2024\n\n2\n\nDepartment of Mathematics, Princeton University,\nWashington Road, Princeton NJ 08544, United States of America\n\nFebruary 15, 2024\n\nAbstract\nIn this paper, we prove that extremal black holes arise on the threshold of gravitational collapse.\nMore precisely, we construct smooth one-parameter families of smooth, spherically symmetric solutions\nto the Einstein–Maxwell–Vlasov system which interpolate between dispersion and collapse and for which\nthe critical solution is an extremal black hole. Physically, these solutions can be understood as beams of\ngravitationally self-interacting collisionless charged particles fired into Minkowski space from past infinity.\nDepending on the precise value of the parameter, we show that the Vlasov matter either disperses due\nto the combined effects of angular momentum and electromagnetic repulsion, or undergoes gravitational\ncollapse. At the critical value of the parameter, an extremal Reissner–Nordström black hole is formed. It reports `0 need cache / 0 missing-from-registry`.\n\nIt cannot see the D3 gaps below, by design: `scripts/validation/checks/citations.py:565` and `:389` resolve caches by **globbing `Lit-Search/*/primary-sources/<bibkey>.{pdf,tex,abstract.txt,json}`**, not by the registry's `primary_source_path`. `.json` is an accepted cache extension, and only `.abstract.txt` gets flagged as abstract-only.
